Benefits of Strategic Site Selection 
1.​ Sustainable Urban Growth: By focusing on areas that are safe from natural hazards and unsuitable for agriculture, Bamban can promote sustainable 
urban growth. This approach not only helps protect the environment but also allows for the efficient use of land resources. 
2.​ Enhanced Community Resilience: Developing in locations that are less vulnerable to natural disasters strengthens the resilience of the community. This 
proactive planning can lead to safer neighborhoods and better preparedness for potential environmental challenges. 
3.​ Economic Opportunities: Transforming these underutilized areas into residential, commercial, or mixed-use developments can stimulate local economies. 
The creation of new housing, businesses, and public spaces can attract investments and create jobs, contributing to the overall economic growth of the 
municipality. 
4.​ Improved Quality of Life: Strategically planned developments can enhance the quality of life for residents by providing access to essential services, 
recreational areas, and community facilities. Thoughtful urban planning ensures that neighborhoods are well-connected, pedestrian-friendly, and 
conducive to social interaction. 
5.​ Biodiversity Preservation: Utilizing areas that are no longer viable for agriculture allows the municipality to preserve more fertile agricultural lands for 
food production, protecting local ecosystems and promoting biodiversity. This balance between development and conservation is crucial for long-term 
sustainability. 
The careful identification and selection of potential development sites in Bamban during this planning period is a forward-thinking strategy that prioritizes safety, 
sustainability, and community well-being. By focusing on areas that are resilient to flooding and landslides and no longer suitable for crop cultivation, the 
municipality can create thriving, sustainable communities that contribute to the overall growth and prosperity of the region. This approach not only addresses 
current needs but also lays the foundation for a safer and more prosperous future for all residents. 
 
1.14.1.2.​
4PH project in a planning standpoint medium rise development for housing projects are preferable than sprawling 
The Pambansang Pabahay para sa Pilipino Program (National Housing Program for Filipinos) presents a significant opportunity for the Local Government Unit 
(LGU) of Bamban to effectively manage and enhance the residential component of the municipality. This program aims to provide affordable housing solutions to 
Filipino families, particularly those in need, thereby addressing the growing demand for residential units. 
 
 
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

===== PAGE 166 =====

Strategic Advantages of Medium-Rise Development 
1.​ Efficient Land Use: Medium-rise developments, typically defined as buildings ranging from four to ten stories, are an ideal solution for maximizing the 
limited buildable areas in Bamban. Given the constraints of available land and the municipality's vulnerability to flooding and landslides, medium-rise 
structures can provide more housing units per square meter compared to traditional low-rise developments. This efficient use of space helps to 
accommodate a larger population while preserving open spaces and minimizing environmental impact. 
2.​ Affordable Housing Options: By participating in the Pambansang Pabahay program, the LGU can develop medium-rise residential projects that offer 
affordable housing options for various income levels. This is particularly important in a municipality where economic disparities may exist. Providing 
affordable housing helps to improve the living conditions of lower-income families and promotes social equity. 
3.​ Community Integration: Medium-rise developments can facilitate the creation of integrated communities that include residential, commercial, and 
recreational spaces. By combining these elements, the LGU can foster vibrant neighborhoods where residents have easy access to essential services, schools, 
parks, and local businesses. This integration promotes a sense of community and enhances the quality of life for residents. 
4.​ Sustainable Urban Development: Emphasizing medium-rise development aligns with sustainable urban planning principles. Such projects often 
incorporate green building practices, energy-efficient designs, and communal spaces that promote social interaction. This approach can contribute to 
reducing the municipality's carbon footprint and enhancing environmental sustainability. 
5.​ Infrastructure Efficiency: Medium-rise buildings often encourage better utilization of existing infrastructure, including roads, public transportation, and 
utilities. This can lead to improved service delivery and reduced costs for the municipality. Additionally, increased density in residential areas can support 
local businesses and stimulate economic activity. 
6.​ Disaster Resilience: Developing medium-rise buildings with proper engineering and design can enhance the community's resilience to natural disasters. 
Elevated structures can be designed to withstand flooding and landslides, ensuring the safety of residents and minimizing property damage during adverse 
weather conditions. 
The Pambansang Pabahay para sa Pilipino Program offers the Local Government Unit of Bamban a strategic opportunity to manage and expand the 
municipality's residential sector through medium-rise developments. This approach not only maximizes limited buildable areas but also addresses the critical need 
for affordable housing, promotes community integration, and supports sustainable urban development. By leveraging this program, Bamban can enhance the 
quality of life for its residents while contributing to the overall growth and resilience of the municipality.

1.15.​ Functional Role 
The Municipality of Bamban serves as a gateway for economic growth in the Province of Tarlac, aligning with the Central Luzon Physical Development 
Framework. As part of Strategic Growth Area 1, Bamban plays a crucial role as a regional center, capitalizing on its strategic location and connectivity 
to major transportation networks. 
As an integral part of the Metro Clark Area, Bamban benefits from its proximity to key infrastructure, including the North Luzon Expressway (NLEX), 
Subic-Clark-Tarlac Expressway (SCTEX), Tarlac-Pangasinan-La Union Expressway (TPLEX), Manila North Road, and the forthcoming 
North-South Commuter Railway. Positioned between Clark Freeport Zone, home to Clark International Airport, and New Clark City, Bamban is 
well-placed to support and complement the region’s expanding economic activities. 
Strategic Land Use Planning 
The proposed land use plan is designed to complement ongoing regional economic initiatives, ensuring that developments in Bamban integrate 
supportive and complementary land uses rather than competing with existing economic zones. 
Residential Expansion 
With increasing employment opportunities in the region, Bamban is expected to experience a surge in population growth. Notably, projections for New 
Clark City estimate a future population of approximately 2 million once fully developed. To accommodate this anticipated influx, the land use plan 
includes designated residential expansion areas, ensuring sufficient housing supply while preventing overcrowding and congestion in neighboring 
municipalities. 
Provincial Role of Bamban 
Beyond its regional significance, Bamban plays a key role in Tarlac’s Eco-Tourism Cluster. This classification has influenced land use planning by striking a 
balance between agricultural preservation and sustainable tourism development. 
Eco-Tourism and Agricultural Preservation 
To support local farmers while maintaining productive agricultural lands, the municipality promotes agri-tourism initiatives, such as:

●​ Educational farm tours and demonstration farms​
 
●​ Organic markets and farm-to-table restaurants​
 
By integrating eco-tourism within its agricultural landscape, Bamban preserves farmlands while creating alternative income opportunities for local farmers, 
thereby strengthening the rural economy. 
Logistics and Agri-Processing Hub 
Bamban is also a major player in Tarlac’s Distribution and Logistics Hub/Agri-Processing Cluster. To align with this role, the municipality has carefully 
planned the relocation of existing agri-industrial developments along the National Highway, ensuring improved efficiency and reduced congestion. 
Additionally, the land use plan incorporates a Transport-Oriented Development (TOD) approach, which enhances logistics infrastructure and 
streamlines transport connectivity across the province. By integrating strategically located logistics hubs, Bamban is poised to strengthen the distribution 
network and supply chain of the Tarlac logistics industry, fostering economic resilience and sustainable growth.

1.16.1 Production Area Policies 
 
Agricultural and Production Area Policies 
Resource Allocation & Crop Diversification 
●​ Prioritize funding and technical support to barangays engaged in agriculture. 
●​ Broaden crop varieties beyond the current 60 % rice dominance to boost income resiliency and biodiversity

●​ Encourage full utilization of irrigated and well-connected farmlands to maximize production efficiency.

●​ Merge watershed management, soil erosion control, and agricultural planning into a unified environmental‑agriculture policy. 
​
Post‑Harvest Value‑Adding 
●​ Boost local processing of farm products to reduce losses, prolong shelf-life, and increase market value. 
Hazard‑Prone Area Protection 
Map agricultural lands at risk from hazards (e.g., flooding, landslides) and regulate development through legislation.

1.16.2 Settlement Area Policies 
 
●​ Embrace cluster development by organizing residential zones into compact villages or neighborhoods, anchored by local centers offering essential 
services—a proven strategy to preserve open space and foster community life.  
●​ Define a clear urban hierarchy—from neighborhood clusters (village scale) to town centers—to structure growth and distribute services efficiently.  
●​ Apply New Urbanism principles to create walkable, mixed-use communities where people can live, work, shop, dine, worship, and play within a short 
distance of home . 
●​ Design street networks for connectivity and permeability, avoiding cul‑de‑sacs and encouraging multiple pedestrian, bicycle, and vehicle routes . 
●​ Ensure neighborhoods are compact—within a 5–10 minute walk to a neighborhood center—supporting daily short-distance living 
●​ Introduce PUD zoning to encourage flexible, mixed-use layouts that cluster housing, open spaces, and small-scale commerce—reducing infrastructure 
footprints and promoting walkability 
●​ Use PUDs as a tool to manage densities, preserve green spaces, and embed amenities (e.g., retail shops, offices, civic spaces) within communities. 
●​ Foster urban forms that minimize through-car traffic in residential zones through limited access roads and street design that slows vehicles 
●​ Integrate pedestrian-first design, featuring narrow streets, sidewalk buffers, on‑street parking, and shaded walkways, which support casual social 
interaction and discourage speeding .

2.2.​ Development Strategies 
To achieve the development goals, several spatial strategies must be applied and implemented through a concept and structure plan and later, through the zoning 
ordinance and zoning map. 
 
2.2.1.​
National Spatial Strategy 
The three components of the National Spatial Strategy (NSS)3 may be adopted as local spatial strategies. These components are: 
1.​ Concentration of development. This strategy minimizes sprawl or haphazard horizontal development by concentrating development in growth nodes 
or growth centers. Sprawl puts pressure on agricultural land (leading to premature conversions and conversion of prime agricultural land), which in turn, 
puts pressure on forestland (leading to deforestation). Sprawl increases GHG emissions from transportation, construction, and other human activities, 
while deforestation reduces the natural carbon capture and storage potential of the municipality and country. Concentration addresses both the climate 
change and biodiversity crises. 
2.​ Connectivity of urban spaces. This strategy involves the construction of climate-adaptive and disaster-resilient roads, bridges, transportation terminals, 
and other connective infrastructure to ensure the efficient flow of people, goods, and services within the municipality as well as to and from the 
municipality. Improving the access to communities and creating redundancies in access also improves disaster response. 
3.​ Reduction of vulnerability of communities. This strategy involves the relocation of families living in danger zones into safe, accessible, 
climate-adaptive, disaster-resilient, and decent houses. In Bamban, this includes areas that are highly susceptible to flooding and landslide. This strategy 
also involves the upgrading of houses made of light materials into stronger, disaster-resilient materials. It also involves the protection of water bodies such 
as Baldo Creek and Susubaen Creek (from sedimentation, blockage, and erosion) to prevent the risk of flooding. 
 
All three strategies are essential in realizing a safer future for the residents of the municipality. 
 
3 This strategy is discussed in depth in the Philippine Development Plan for 2017-2022 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​

===== PAGE 180 =====

​

2.2.2.​
Densification Strategy 
The NSS strategy of concentrating urban development is consistent with the Densification Strategy or Vertical Development Strategy wherein developments are 
encouraged to go upwards (additional floor levels) instead of sidewards (additional land, usually vacant agricultural land). While the Concentration Strategy aims 
to reduce sprawl, the Densification or Vertical Development Strategy aims to maximize the use of land by increasing gross floor area (GFA) within the limits set by 
the Zoning Ordinance or the National Building Code on lots, depending on the zone. These limits include building height, percentage of site occupancy (PSO), 
and floor-to-lot area ratio (FLAR) or floor area ratio (FAR).  
 
From the lot owner or developer's perspective, densification maximizes profit over the total development costs and the current market value of land. From the 
perspective of business locators, densification allows them to locate closer to suppliers and customers. From the perspective of residents, densification allows them 
to live closer to work, to shops, and to other places of interest. This is especially true in CBDs and in certain PUDs4. Residents can also live closer to public 
transportation such as in TODs5. From the government's perspective, more taxes can be generated from the same unit of land through increased property tax, 
business tax, and income taxes. Densification creates more jobs and other economic opportunities per unit of land. 
 
2.2.3.​
Natural Conservation Strategy 
The Concentration Strategy is also complementary to the Natural Conservation Strategy wherein natural environments are protected from the expansion of 
human settlements. In Bamban, the areas for conservation are the wetlands, the agricultural lands, the Susubaen Creek, and Baldo Creek. 
 
Provisioning Services of the Natural Environment: 
1.​ Source of food 
2.​ Source of clothing materials 
3.​ Source of shelter materials 
4.​ Source of medicine 
5.​ Source of water, recharges city aquifers 
5 Transit-oriented development, or TOD, includes a mix of commercial, residential, office and entertainment centered around or located near a transit station. (Source: 
https://www.transit.dot.gov/TOD) 
4 A PUD is a land development scheme wherein the project site is comprehensively planned as an entity through a single comprehensive master development plan which permits flexibility in 
planning/design, building siting, complementarity of building types and land uses, usable open spaces, and the preservation of significant natural land features. (DHSUD) 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

2.3.​ Development Concept and Structure Plan 
A Concept and Structure Plan (CSP) is a diagram that shows major centers, major land uses, and major circulation networks in a planning area. In architecture, 
this is similar to the bubble diagram, a freehand drawing of spaces (represented by bubbles) and pathways (represented by solid lines) used to show the 
relationship and access between spaces. In the CSP for the municipality, major centers are marked by red circles, major land uses are represented by “bubbles”, and 
major thoroughfares are represented by solid black lines with arrowheads, all overlaid on the municipality’s base map (showing built-up areas, roads, waterways, 
vegetation, and topographic contours).  
 
The CSP shall serve as the basis for the preparation of the Proposed Land Use Map.

2.3.2.​
Preferred Option 
After a thorough exploration of the advantages and disadvantages of various proposed CSP options, workshop participants decisively chose the Multi-Nodal and 
Radial Development Plan as their preferred urban form. This selection underscores a strong commitment to fostering a vibrant and adaptable urban 
environment, defined by multiple interconnected hubs, or "nodes." These nodes serve as focal points for activity, enhancing accessibility for residents and 
promoting lively community interactions. 
The radial design of this plan significantly improves connectivity within the urban landscape, allowing for efficient transportation systems that minimize 
congestion and streamline travel. By ensuring that each node is easily accessible, the plan encourages walking, cycling, and the use of public transit, thereby 
reducing reliance on cars and lowering environmental impacts. Furthermore, this development strategy supports a diverse range of land uses within each node, 
integrating residential, commercial, and recreational spaces to create a well-rounded community experience. 
In alignment with the new vision and development goals, the Multi-Nodal and Radial Development Plan emphasizes sustainable growth principles. It prioritizes 
mixed-use developments, which not only facilitate economic activity but also enhance social cohesion by bringing people together in shared spaces. Additionally, 
the inclusion of green spaces throughout the nodes contributes to residents' well-being and environmental health, fostering a sense of community and connection 
to nature. 
Ultimately, by adopting this innovative urban framework, the community seeks to cultivate a more cohesive and resilient urban landscape. This approach not 
only addresses current housing and infrastructure needs but also anticipates future growth and demographic changes, ensuring that the urban environment 
remains adaptable and capable of meeting the evolving needs of its residents. Through thoughtful planning and collaboration, the community is poised to create 
a thriving urban ecosystem that enhances quality of life for all its inhabitants.

2.4.​ Proposed Land Use Plan 
Regional Role of the Municipality of Bamban 
The proposed land use plan begins with a comprehensive assessment of the Municipality of Bamban’s role in Region III. As an integral part of the Metro Clark 
Area, which is considered a rapidly emerging conurbation of Strategic Growth Areas, Bamban occupies a pivotal position due to its proximity to major 
transportation networks. These include the North Luzon Expressway (NLEX), Subic-Clark-Tarlac Expressway (SCTEX), Tarlac-Pangasinan-La Union 
Expressway (TPLEX), the Manila North Road, and the forthcoming North-South Commuter Railway. 
Strategically positioned between two of the country’s major economic zones—the Clark Freeport Zone in the south, which hosts the Clark International Airport, 
and New Clark City in the north—the municipality is poised to benefit from increasing economic activity. With these developments in mind, the land use 
planning process has been carefully designed to avoid conflicts with existing regional economic initiatives. Instead of introducing land uses that might compete 
with ongoing developments, the planning team has focused on integrating complementary uses that enhance the region’s economic dynamics. 
Transport-Oriented Development (TOD) Zone 
A key feature of the proposed land use plan is the introduction of a Transport-Oriented Development (TOD) Zone, designed in anticipation of the North-South 
Commuter Railway. This zone is expected to support the establishment of an intermodal transport hub within the municipality. By concentrating 
transport-related facilities in this area, the TOD Zone will cater to a range of developments, including: 
●​ Park-and-fly facilities 
●​ Hotels and transient accommodations 
●​ Commercial establishments 
●​ Automotive services, including vehicle care and maintenance industries 
These initiatives aim to enhance mobility and connectivity while fostering sustainable urban growth in the municipality. 
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
187 


===== PAGE 191 =====
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
   
 
Residential Expansion 
As economic zones restrict private property ownership, a surge in population growth is expected due to increasing job opportunities in the region. To 
accommodate this influx, the proposed land use plan includes provisions for expanding residential areas. These developments will ensure that incoming workers 
have adequate housing options within Bamban, reducing congestion and urban sprawl in neighboring areas. 
Provincial Role of the Municipality of Bamban 
Beyond its regional significance, Bamban plays a crucial role at the provincial level within Tarlac’s Eco-Tourism Cluster. This classification has shaped key 
elements of the land use plan, particularly in safeguarding the agricultural sector while promoting sustainable tourism development. 
Eco-Tourism and Agricultural Preservation 
Recognizing the struggles of local farmers in maintaining their livelihoods, the municipality has incorporated tourism components within agricultural lands. This 
strategic approach not only preserves farmlands but also enhances their economic viability by integrating agritourism initiatives such as: 
●​ Farm resorts and agri-tourism parks 
●​ Educational farm tours and demonstration farms 
●​ Organic markets and farm-to-table restaurants 
By leveraging eco-tourism, Bamban can generate supplementary income streams for farmers while boosting the local economy. 
Logistics and Agri-Processing Hub 
Bamban is also a key player in the province’s Distribution and Logistics Hub/Agri-Processing Cluster. To align with this role, careful planning has been 
undertaken regarding the relocation of existing agri-industrial developments along the National Highway. 
The proposed Light Industrial Zones have been strategically positioned in the southeastern part of the municipality, adjacent to similar developments in 
Mabalacat. To mitigate potential adverse impacts on surrounding areas, buffer zones have been designated: 
●​ A 20.00m buffer zone along the peripheries of Light Industrial Developments to ensure a transition between industrial and non-industrial zones. 
●​ A 15.00m buffer zone along agricultural areas near the municipal boundaries, particularly in Capas and Concepcion, to preserve farmland and maintain 
environmental integrity. 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

Land Use Computation and Allocation 
Following an assessment of Bamban’s regional and provincial roles, a comprehensive computation of projected land use demand was conducted. This involved: 
1.​ Land Demand Analysis – Determining the required land allocations for residential, commercial, industrial, institutional, and agricultural uses based on 
population growth projections and economic trends. 
2.​ Development Concept Formulation – Establishing a framework for land use distribution that aligns with Bamban’s strategic development vision. 
3.​ Structure Planning – Mapping out transportation networks, infrastructure provisions, and urban growth corridors. 
4.​ Decision Areas from the Climate and Disaster Risk Assessment (CDRA) – Integrating resilience planning to mitigate climate change impacts and 
disaster risks. 
5.​ Final Land Allocation – Distributing land areas across newly defined land use categories to balance economic growth, environmental sustainability, and 
social development. 
The resulting land use plan ensures that Bamban remains a dynamic and resilient municipality, fostering sustainable growth while preserving its natural and 
economic assets. Through strategic zoning and infrastructure planning, the municipality is poised to harness its full potential as a key player in both regional and 
provincial development. 
 
2.4.1.​
Protection 
Agricultural lands that must be protected include those that are irrigated, irrigable, or the conversion of which will negatively impact ecosystems downstream. 
 
With a projected population of 100,376 by 2031, the municipality will require a total rice land area of 2,407.26 hectares to adequately feed its people. Note that 
the computation in Table 2.4.1 is only for cereal and cereal products. A buffer of 50% may be sufficient to cover both the land requirement for rice, vegetables, 
and other agricultural produce (a total of 4,706.80 hectares).

2.4.4.​
Residential 
Most of the residential areas located within the military reservation and Certificate of Ancestral Domain Title (CADT) regions have been reverted to forest land 
use. This strategic decision aims to protect the Bamban forest ecosystem, a vital area rich in biodiversity, cultural heritage, and ancestral significance. By restoring 
these lands to their original forest status, the initiative not only safeguards critical habitats for various flora and fauna but also upholds the rights of Indigenous 
communities, ensuring that their ancestral connections to the land are respected and preserved. 
The reversion of these areas to forest land serves multiple purposes. It enhances ecological integrity by preventing deforestation, soil erosion, and habitat 
destruction, which are crucial for maintaining water quality and climate resilience. The restored forests also function as carbon sinks, contributing to global and 
local climate change mitigation efforts. Additionally, this approach aligns with broader conservation goals by protecting endemic and endangered species that 
inhabit the Bamban forest ecosystem. 
Recognizing the cultural and historical significance of these lands, the reversion process is designed to respect and integrate Indigenous knowledge and practices 
in forest conservation. Traditional ecological practices of the Aeta and other Indigenous groups play a critical role in sustainable land stewardship, ensuring that 
the forests remain a thriving and protected landscape for future generations. Moreover, heritage conservation efforts will focus on preserving sacred sites, 
historical landmarks, and traditional land-use patterns that define the identity of the Indigenous communities in Bamban. 
At the same time, the Municipality of Bamban is strategically positioned at the heart of two major economic zones, including the highly anticipated New Clark 
City, which is projected to generate approximately 600,000 jobs and support a population of over 1.2 million residents. This significant economic development 
creates a robust demand for residential housing, and Bamban is uniquely situated to meet this need. As New Clark City and other surrounding economic zones 
primarily offer long-term leases for properties, they may not be able to accommodate the immediate housing requirements of the workforce and residents who 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

​

will migrate to the area for job opportunities. Consequently, many individuals and families will likely seek residential options in nearby municipalities, with 
Bamban emerging as a prime choice due to its proximity and accessibility. 
The anticipated influx of residents underscores the importance of integrating biodiversity conservation, heritage preservation, and ancestral domain rights into 
comprehensive urban planning. This includes infrastructure improvements, such as sustainable transportation networks, environmentally conscious housing 
developments, and essential public services like schools, healthcare facilities, and recreational areas. Sustainable development strategies will prioritize eco-friendly 
urban expansion, ensuring that new residential areas harmonize with the natural environment and respect Indigenous cultural landscapes. 
Furthermore, this residential growth presents opportunities for local businesses and service providers to thrive, contributing to the overall economic vitality of the 
municipality. By leveraging its strategic location and responding proactively to housing demand while safeguarding biodiversity and cultural heritage, the 
Municipality of Bamban can position itself as a model for sustainable and inclusive development. This approach fosters a harmonious balance between economic 
progress, environmental stewardship, and the protection of ancestral lands, ensuring a resilient and thriving future for both human and natural communities.

2.4.4.1.​
Socialized Housing 
A Local Shelter Plan (LSP) or a Community-Based Monitoring System (CBMS) survey should be able to indicate the number of households that should be 
provided with socialized housing. 
 
Based on the 2022 CBMS the average population size per household is 3.89, and the projected population for 2031, the housing demand (assuming there are no 
doubled-up housing) is 19,042 for 2022 and 23,412 for 2031. With the existing supply of 17,821 houses made of strong construction materials), the current 
requirement (2022) is 1,220 and the future requirement (2031) is 15,166. 
 
This indicates an average annual increase in the number of houses of 486 units from 2022 to 2031.

2.4.6.​
Agri-Industrial 
During a series of planning workshops and public hearings, stakeholders strongly suggested that certain agri-industrial developments be converted into more 
suitable land uses to accommodate economically viable projects. This recommendation stems from a recognition of the evolving economic landscape and the 
need for adaptability in land use planning. 
As the demand for diverse economic opportunities grows, some existing agri-industrial sites may no longer be the most effective use of valuable land. By 
repurposing these areas, the community can foster the development of sectors that align more closely with current market trends and local needs, such as 
technology parks, retail centers, or mixed-use developments that combine residential, commercial, and recreational spaces. 
Transitioning from agri-industrial to other uses can also help stimulate local economies by attracting new businesses and creating jobs. For instance, converting 
underutilized agricultural sites into logistics hubs or light manufacturing facilities could capitalize on Bamban's strategic location and connectivity to major 
economic zones. This shift not only opens up avenues for job creation but also enhances the municipality's overall economic resilience. 
Additionally, repurposing agri-industrial areas may provide opportunities for sustainable development initiatives. For example, integrating green technologies or 
sustainable practices in new developments can contribute to environmental goals, improve energy efficiency, and promote a healthier community. 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

Overall, the community's call for these conversions reflects a proactive approach to urban planning, aiming to balance economic growth with the responsible use 
of land resources. By embracing change and exploring new possibilities, Bamban can better position itself for a prosperous future that meets the needs of its 
residents and businesses alike. 
 
2.4.7.​
Industrial 
The FAO standards are typically used to determine the demand for industrial land (including Agri-industrial land). For every 1,000 population, the range of 
industrial land should be between 0.40 to 1.50 hectares. Alternatively, the demand for industrial land may be determined through the DHSUD standards: (1) for 
Light Industrial, 0.80 hectares for every 1,000 population, (2) for Medium Industrial, 2.50 ha for every 1,000 population, and (3) for Heavy Industrial, 4.00 ha 
for every 1,000 population.  
 
With a projected population of 100,376 by 2031, the municipality will require a total industrial land area of between 40.15 to 80.30 hectares using the FAO 
standards and DHSUD CLUP Guidebook Standards.

2.4.9.​
Tourism 
There is no standard for the required land area for tourism activities.  
 
Eco-tourism projects and activities may be allowed within and along streams, provided that:  
●​ These projects and activities are identified or aligned with the municipality’s Local Tourism Development Plan. 
●​ No significant air, water, or solid waste pollution will be generated from the projects or activities and any wastes are properly managed based on a plan. 
●​ The flow of water is not impeded by the projects or activities. 
 
2.4.10.​
Parks and Recreation 
DHSUD standards are typically used to determine the demand for parks and recreational land. For every 1,000 population, there should be 500 sq. m. of parks 
and optionally, an additional 5,000 sq. m. of playfields (e.g., track and field, baseball diamond).  
 
With a projected population of 100,376 by 2031, the municipality will require a total park area of 3.68 hectares. Since the municipality has 1.34 hectares of park 
areas, it needs an additional 2.24 hectares for parks. Should it opt to have an area for playfields, an estimate of 47.41 hectares will be needed by 2031.

2.4.11.​
Cemetery/Memorial Park 
There is no additional land requirement for a cemetery/memorial park in Bamban. The existing land area for the cemetery of 7.37  hectares is still sufficient to 
cover future demands. 
 
2.4.12.​
Buffer/Greenbelt 
The demand for buffer/greenbelt areas is not typically projected. Delineating these areas is based on the Water Code of the Philippines which requires an 
easement from the edge of water bodies of 3 meters if within urban areas, 20 meters if within agricultural areas, and 40 meters if within forest areas. 
 
2.4.13.​
Utilities, Transportation, and Services 
Computing the demand for UTS is complex as it includes various components: roads, bridges, terminals, MRF and other solid waste facilities, flood control 
facilities, etc.

2.6.​ Proposed Land Use Distribution 
2.6.1.​
Forest 
The current forest area spans 27,445.77 hectares. Under the proposed land use and zoning map this will reduce to 27,367.01 hectares, divided into a 
23,230.60-hectare forest reserve and a 4,136.41-hectare special-use forest—a decrease of 78.76 has. 
To safeguard the forest zone from broader urban or industrial development, the plan categorizes several residential, commercial, and resort projects under the 
forest designation. This allows only low-impact, supportive developments—strictly limited to the uses that reinforce forest protection—per the zoning 
ordinance. 
     
2.6.2.​
Agriculture 
The current agricultural land in the municipality, totaling 3,352 hectares, is proposed to be reduced by approximately 10%. This reduction reflects a strategic shift 
in land use planning aimed at enhancing economic viability while still recognizing the importance of agriculture in the community. 
To better manage and utilize the remaining agricultural land, it is proposed that it be classified into two distinct categories: Protection Agricultural Areas and 
Production Agricultural Areas. 
1.​ Protection Agricultural Areas: These zones are designated to conserve land that plays a critical role in maintaining the ecological balance, preserving 
biodiversity, and protecting vital agricultural resources. This classification helps ensure that the most valuable agricultural lands are safeguarded from 
urban development and other encroachments, allowing them to continue serving their environmental and community functions. 
2.​ Production Agricultural Areas: In contrast, these areas focus on land actively utilized for agricultural production. By emphasizing productive use, the 
municipality can support local farmers and agricultural businesses, fostering food security and promoting sustainable agricultural practices. This 
classification can also encourage the adoption of modern farming techniques, enhancing productivity and profitability for farmers. 
Additionally, the proposed zoning of approximately 334.90 hectares of agricultural land to other economically feasible uses signifies a proactive response to the 
changing economic landscape. By transitioning these areas to uses such as residential, commercial, or mixed-use developments, the municipality aims to attract 
investment, create jobs, and stimulate economic growth. This shift aligns with the broader goals of urban development while recognizing that not all agricultural 
land is equally suited for farming in the context of a rapidly evolving economy. 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
200 


===== PAGE 204 =====
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
   
 
Overall, this proposed reclassification and reduction in agricultural land signify a balanced approach to land use, aiming to protect essential agricultural resources 
while also promoting economic development that benefits the community as a whole. This dual strategy can help ensure that Bamban remains resilient and 
adaptable in the face of future challenges and opportunities. 
 
 
2.6.3.​
Waterways 
For all existing creeks and waterways within Bamban, a standard easement of 3.00 meters will be enforced. This easement serves as a buffer zone, providing 
essential protection against potential encroachments and ensuring that the waterways remain unpolluted and accessible for future generations. This measure aims 
to maintain the health of these ecosystems, promoting biodiversity and water quality. 
In addition, the municipality will establish a more substantial easement of 20.00 meters for the Sacobia River and the Tarlac River. These rivers are significant not 
only for their ecological value but also for their contribution to the local economy, recreation, and community well-being. The wider easement reflects the 
importance of these waterways and aims to safeguard them from urban development and environmental degradation. 
By implementing these easement regulations, the Municipality of Bamban is taking proactive steps to protect its natural resources, promote sustainable land use, 
and enhance the quality of life for its residents. This initiative aligns with broader environmental goals, ensuring that the waterways continue to thrive while 
serving the needs of the community. Through careful management and protection of these areas, Bamban can preserve its natural heritage and foster a healthier 
environment for all. 
2.6.4.​
Aquaculture 
On the 1999–2001 Comprehensive Land Use Plan, there were no areas designated for aquaculture. However, by 2016, office research documented 56.31 
hectares under aquaculture. A 2022 field survey, however, found just 2.98 hectares being actively used—signifying a dramatic 95% decline in aquaculture 
area over that period. 
2.6.5.​
Residential 
A decrease of 606.12, equivalent to 54% reduction in residential land has occurred. This reduction is attributed to two (2) primary factors: (1) the rezoning of 
buffer and greenbelt areas, which typically restricts the construction and development in environmentally sensitive areas, and (2) the proposed relocation of 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​

===== PAGE 205 =====

​
​
​

households living along waterways and identified risk areas in the municipality. These combined actions have led to a significant reduction in the available land for 
residential purposes.  
 
The proposed residential land area is 516.12 hectares, reduced from 606.12 hectares, and is now zoned as Residential‑3 (R‑3). This reclassification aims to 
maximize land use by enabling vertical housing developments, thereby increasing housing capacity, optimizing land efficiency, and better meeting Bamban’s 
growing needs. 
 
The following are the rationale for consolidating the residential areas and the resulting “disappearance” of residential areas in the proposed land use map 
compared to the existing land use map. 
 
●​ Protection of agricultural land. Allowing scattered residential zones within agricultural lands will threaten food security with the expansion of housing. 
The expansion of residential areas within agricultural lands is therefore discouraged. 
●​ The relocation of houses away from areas that are highly susceptible to flooding is strongly encouraged. 
●​ Please note that scattered residential areas do not constitute a zone for which boundaries can be delineated, defined, or described.  
●​ Although residential areas decreased, please note that houses are allowed in agricultural zones (farm households) as well as in commercial zones (along 
highways).  
●​ The height limit for residential zones and the listed allowable zones both encourage the development of multi-family dwelling units to minimize sprawl 
into agricultural lands and encourage vertical development in safer areas. 
●​ "Concentration" and "Reduction of Vulnerability" are two of the three National Spatial Strategies in the previous PDP (2017-2022). The plan 
concentrates residential areas into barangay propers, near road access, etc., all away from areas that are highly susceptible to flooding and away from prime 
agricultural land. 
●​ Also please note that residential developments are allowed in commercial zones and these zones are expanded along certain sections of major 
thoroughfares. 
 
2.6.6.​
Socialized Housing 
A key focus during this planning period for the 4PH project is the strategic allocation of significant land areas to meet the anticipated demand for housing. With 
projections indicating that the population will grow to a total of 41,317 households by 2031, it is crucial to approach this development with careful consideration 
of infrastructure, amenities, and community services.

Strategic Land Allocation​
The careful selection of land for residential development will not only accommodate the growing population but also ensure that housing is accessible and 
integrated within the community. This involves identifying suitable locations that can support new residential areas while also considering environmental factors, 
transportation links, and proximity to essential services. 
Infrastructure Development​
To effectively support the projected increase in households, infrastructure must be a top priority. This includes transportation systems—such as roads, public 
transit, and pedestrian pathways—that facilitate easy movement throughout the community. Additionally, utilities like water, electricity, and waste management 
systems must be adequately planned to handle the increased demand. Investing in resilient infrastructure ensures that the community can withstand future 
challenges, such as climate change or population fluctuations. 
Amenities and Community Services​
Beyond basic infrastructure, the integration of amenities is vital for enhancing livability. This includes parks, recreational facilities, schools, healthcare services, 
and shopping areas, which contribute to a high quality of life. By creating spaces where residents can gather and interact, the development can foster a sense of 
community and belonging, making Bamban not just a place to live but a vibrant environment where people thrive. 
Sustainable Growth​
This proactive approach to planning aims to support sustainable growth that balances economic development with environmental stewardship. Sustainable 
practices—such as green building initiatives, energy-efficient designs, and the preservation of open spaces—will help minimize the ecological footprint of new 
developments. By prioritizing sustainability, the community can protect natural resources and promote long-term resilience against environmental challenges. 
Addressing Evolving Needs​
Planning thoughtfully for the increase in households also means being responsive to the evolving needs of the population. This involves engaging with 
community members to understand their aspirations and concerns, ensuring that the development reflects the diverse interests of residents. By incorporating 
feedback into the planning process, the project can adapt to demographic changes, such as aging populations or shifts in family structures, ultimately creating 
inclusive communities. 
In summary, by strategically allocating land, investing in robust infrastructure, integrating essential amenities, and embracing sustainable practices, the 4PH 
project can cultivate vibrant and resilient communities that enhance the well-being of residents while preserving the environment. This comprehensive approach 
not only addresses current housing demands but also positions Bamban for a prosperous and sustainable future.

2.6.7.​
Commercial 
The existing land use in Bamban currently features over 219.81 hectares dedicated to commercial developments. However, the proposed Land Use Plan outlines 
an expansion to 486.55 hectares, reflecting a strategic vision to enhance commercial activity throughout the municipality. This increase is largely driven by the 
establishment of new commercial developments along major roads, which are critical corridors for economic activity and connectivity. 
Expansion of Commercial Developments​
The planned expansion of commercial areas is designed to accommodate a diverse range of businesses, from retail and dining to services and entertainment. By 
strategically positioning these developments along major transportation routes, the municipality can ensure high visibility and accessibility, making it easier for 
residents and visitors to engage with these commercial spaces. This approach not only supports local entrepreneurs and businesses but also attracts larger retailers, 
boosting the local economy and creating job opportunities. 
Institutional Developments and the “University Town Concept”​
In addition to general commercial growth, the planning period has placed significant emphasis on integrating commercial support facilities for institutional 
developments. The vision of establishing a “University Town Concept” involves creating a vibrant environment that caters to the needs of students, faculty, and 
university staff. This includes not only educational facilities but also housing, dining options, bookstores, and recreational spaces that foster a sense of 
community. 
By developing this concept, Bamban aims to position itself as a hub for higher education, attracting students from surrounding areas and beyond. The influx of 
students and faculty can lead to increased demand for services, further stimulating economic activity and creating a dynamic atmosphere where learning and 
commerce thrive side by side. 
Creating Magnet Developments​
The overarching goal of these developments is to create magnet attractions that generate economic movements within the municipality. By drawing people into 
Bamban for educational, commercial, and recreational purposes, the municipality can enhance its profile as a desirable destination. This not only benefits existing 
businesses but also encourages new investments, leading to a cycle of growth and development. 
Economic Movements and Community Benefits​
As commercial activities expand, the economic benefits can ripple throughout the community. Increased foot traffic can support local businesses, promote

entrepreneurship, and generate additional tax revenue for the municipality. This revenue can then be reinvested into public services and infrastructure, creating a 
positive feedback loop that enhances the overall quality of life for residents. 
In summary, the proposed Land Use Plan for Bamban aims to significantly expand commercial developments, particularly through the establishment of a 
“University Town Concept.” This strategic approach is designed to attract investment, boost economic activity, and create a vibrant community that meets the 
diverse needs of its residents and visitors. By fostering a thriving commercial environment, Bamban is positioning itself for sustainable growth and a prosperous 
future. 
Is Bamban Ready for this Expansion? 
Bamban’s proposed commercial expansion and the introduction of the “University Town Concept” present a promising vision for economic growth. 
However, successful implementation depends on several key factors, including infrastructure readiness, investment climate, and urban management. 
Below are the justifications assessing Bamban’s readiness for this transformation: 
1. Strategic Location and Connectivity 
Bamban is strategically positioned between Clark Freeport Zone and New Clark City, making it an ideal area for commercial expansion. With 
access to major transportation networks such as NLEX, SCTEX, and TPLEX, the municipality is well-connected to regional and national markets. The 
planned North-South Commuter Railway further strengthens its potential as a commercial hub. These transportation linkages ensure that businesses, 
students, and consumers can easily access commercial areas. 
2. Growing Population and Economic Demand 
The projected population increase to 100,376 by 2030 indicates a growing demand for retail, dining, and services. Additionally, the rise of job 
opportunities in Clark and New Clark City will bring more workers and residents into the area, driving commercial activities. The municipality 
must, however, ensure that housing, transportation, and public services grow in tandem to support this increasing population. 
3. Support for Institutional Development (University Town Concept) 
Bamban’s vision of a University Town aligns with the municipality’s goal of becoming an educational hub. However, this requires significant 
investments in educational infrastructure, housing, and student services. The municipality must coordinate with universities and investors to

4. Investment and Business Climate 
Attracting businesses requires a favorable investment climate, including streamlined business permitting processes, tax incentives, and proper 
zoning regulations. Bamban’s ability to align its land use plan with investor needs will be a determining factor in the success of commercial 
expansion. The municipality must actively engage with private sector stakeholders to ensure smooth and sustainable development. 
5. Infrastructure and Utility Readiness 
A major challenge in supporting large-scale commercial expansion is ensuring adequate infrastructure and utility services, including: 
●​
Road networks and traffic management – To prevent congestion as commercial areas grow.​
 
●​
Water and electricity supply – Increased demand from businesses and residents must be met with adequate utility provisions.​
 
●​
Telecommunications and internet connectivity – A strong digital infrastructure is essential for modern businesses and educational 
institutions.​
 
6. Sustainable Urban Development and Planning 
The increase in commercial areas must not disrupt existing agricultural and residential zones. Proper zoning policies and environmental impact 
assessments should be conducted to balance development with ecological preservation. Bamban must also integrate climate-resilient infrastructure 
and disaster risk reduction into its land use planning.

2.6.8.​
Agri-industrial 
The proposed decrease of  7% in agri-industrial land area in Bamban, reducing from 547.77 hectares to 379.20 hectares, reflects a strategic response to both 
current challenges and future opportunities. This reduction is primarily driven by the prevalence of contagious viral diseases affecting domestic and wild pig 
populations, which has significantly impacted productivity and demand within the agri-industrial sector. 
Impact of Viral Diseases on Productivity​
The outbreak of viral diseases has had a detrimental effect on local livestock, leading to lower production levels and decreased consumer confidence. As a result, 
the economic viability of maintaining large areas dedicated to agri-industrial activities has come into question. This situation necessitates a reassessment of land 
use priorities, prompting a shift away from traditional agricultural practices that may no longer yield sufficient returns. 
Accommodating Economic Growth​
The decision to reduce agri-industrial land is also influenced by the need to facilitate the economic development of New Clark City and other surrounding areas. 
As this major economic zone continues to expand, it offers a wealth of opportunities for growth in various sectors, including retail, services, and technology. By 
reallocating land previously designated for agri-industrial use, Bamban can better position itself to capitalize on the economic benefits that New Clark City and 
associated developments are expected to bring. 
Joint Management Agreement and CADT 025-A​
Additionally, the proposed developments under the Joint Management Agreement Committee for CADT 025-A further emphasize the need for land 
reallocation. This agreement aims to balance the rights and interests of Indigenous communities with economic development initiatives, ensuring that land use 
reflects both cultural heritage and modern economic opportunities. By reducing the agri-industrial footprint, the municipality can create space for developments 
that align with the goals of this agreement, fostering a more inclusive approach to land management. 
New Economic Activities from Clark International Airport​
The anticipated growth in economic activities stemming from the Clark International Airport also plays a crucial role in this land use strategy. As the airport 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

expands its operations and services, it is expected to attract a variety of businesses, from logistics and transportation to tourism and hospitality. This influx of new 
economic activities necessitates additional land for commercial and support developments, further justifying the reduction in agri-industrial land. 
Strategic Land Use Planning​
Overall, the decrease in agri-industrial land area represents a proactive approach to land use planning in Bamban. By reallocating land to better serve emerging 
economic opportunities and address current challenges in the agricultural sector, the municipality can foster sustainable growth that benefits its residents and 
businesses. This strategic realignment aims to create a more resilient economy capable of adapting to changing market conditions while also respecting the 
cultural and ecological context of the area. 
2.6.9.​ Industrial 
The proposed 515% increase in industrial land use — equating to an additional 364.80 hectares — marks a bold and strategic pivot in Bamban’s development 
planning. This expansion reflects the municipality’s proactive response to the growing industrialization in nearby areas, especially in Mabalacat City and the 
Municipality of Concepcion. Below are the key drivers and anticipated impacts of this shift: 
Support for Existing Industrial Growth 
Bamban shares its southern border with Mabalacat City, which is rapidly expanding as a major industrial hub within Metro Clark. By increasing its industrial 
land allocation, Bamban can complement and enhance these developments. The goal is to form a contiguous industrial corridor that leverages shared 
infrastructure, transportation networks, and labor pools—delivering efficiencies and competitive advantage for businesses operating in and around the region. 
Alignment With Concepcion’s Zoning and Regional Growth 
The Municipality of Concepcion has also incorporated industrial zones in its proposed land use plans, particularly in its southwest boundary. To mirror and 
support this regional alignment, a majority of the southeast portion of Bamban is being zoned for industrial use. This spatial coordination ensures that 
cross‑municipality growth is harmonized, reducing friction over land use transitions and contributing to coherent regional planning. 
Attracting New Investments 
With more available industrial land, Bamban positions itself as an attractive destination for new investors across multiple sectors—manufacturing, logistics, 
agro‑processing, technology, warehousing, and related industries. Ample, well‑zoned land with supportive infrastructure will be a strong pull factor for 
companies seeking to locate outside of saturated areas but still within the influence of major transport, gateway, and service corridors. 
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

Economic Development & Job Creation 
The expanded industrial base is expected to generate a significant number of jobs, both in the new industrial zones themselves and via ancillary services (suppliers, 
transport, commerce). This can help reduce unemployment, increase household incomes, and stimulate local businesses, which will benefit from increased 
economic activity and demand. 
 
Infrastructure Enhancement & Improved Connectivity 
To support this growth, investment in backbone infrastructure is essential: new and improved roads, utilities (power, water, telecommunications), drainage, and 
zoning‑friendly transit access. Such improvements not only serve industrial needs but also uplift the quality of life for residents, improving mobility, accessibility, 
and services across the municipality. 
 
Regional Economic Positioning 
By expanding its industrial lands, Bamban strengthens its role in the larger regional economy of Central Luzon. It becomes more than a peripheral municipality; 
rather, it is a partner in regional industrialization, benefitting from spillovers from Mabalacat’s industrial projects (e.g. the Pampanga Technopark) and 
contributing to broader supply chains. 
 
Sustainability and Risk Management 
Industrial expansion brings environmental challenges—pollution, water demand, traffic, waste, etc. Therefore, it is critical that Bamban’s CLUP embeds 
sustainability safeguards: environmental zoning, pollution control, buffer zones near sensitive areas, green infrastructure, regular monitoring, risk assessments, 
and measures for climate resiliency. Ensuring that industrial growth does not degrade ecosystem services (e.g. water supply, air, soil) will help sustain development 
over the long term. 
 
2.6.10.​
Institutional 
Based on the projected student population for the current planning period, the existing land designated for the institutional sector is sufficient to meet the 
current educational demand. However, to stimulate further economic growth and development within the municipality, the introduction of a University Town 
near the metro town center is planned. 
This University Town aims to act as a catalyst for diverse economic activities and development. By establishing a university in this strategic location, the 
municipality anticipates attracting not only students but also faculty, staff, and their families, which will increase local demand for housing, retail, and services. 
This influx can lead to the creation of new businesses, restaurants, and entertainment options, fostering a vibrant community atmosphere.

The university itself will serve as a hub for innovation and research, potentially partnering with local industries and businesses to drive technological

Overall, the establishment of a University Town not only aims to meet educational needs but also seeks to create a dynamic environment that encourages

itself as a progressive and thriving community, ready to embrace the future. 
 
2.6.11.​
Tourism 
There was a 66% decrease in areas allocated for tourism use, primarily due to the rezoning of certain lands into newly designated forest zones. This shift reflects a 
deliberate strategy to curb tourism-related development in sensitive areas, reinforcing environmental protection objectives. 
 
Planning Strategy (see Section 2.6.1)​
 The comprehensive planning approach applied the following key tactics: 
●​ Strategic rezoning: Tourism parcels in ecologically sensitive sites were reclassified as forest zones, aligning land use with conservation goals.​
 
●​ Buffer creation: Forest zoning was employed as a buffer to shield core ecological areas from overdevelopment pressures—an effective precaution seen in 
similar destination planning efforts 
●​ Supportive tourism: Remaining tourism zones were preserved but regulated to ensure that any development is low-impact and ecologically sympathetic.

2.6.12.​
Parks and Recreation 
The current parks and recreation area totals 4.12 hectares. The proposed land-use plan dramatically expands this to 19.63 hectares, representing a 376% 
increase. This substantial growth is fully aligned with the vision articulated in the Comprehensive Land Use Plan: “Enjoy an Ecologically Stable 
Environment.” 
Ecological & Climate Resilience: Parks enhance biodiversity, regulate urban temperatures through shade and evapotranspiration, improve air quality, and 
reduce flood risks by managing stormwater—key functions of “green infrastructure” 
Public Health & Well-Being: Access to green spaces encourages physical activity, alleviates stress, and supports mental health . 
Social Cohesion & Equity: Parks foster community interaction, enhance social inclusion, and serve as gathering spaces for all ages and backgrounds . 
Economic & Environmental Sustainability: Green areas can increase adjacent property values, boost tourism, lower infrastructure and health costs, and serve 
as natural buffers between incompatible land uses . 
 
The proposed expansion supports the municipality’s commitment to ecological stability by: 
-​
Creating an integrated green infrastructure network for enhanced ecosystem services. 
-​
Ensuring multi-functional parks that serve recreation, conservation, education, and disaster preparedness. 
-​
Aligning with “green infrastructure” principles—such as biodiversity corridors, climate adaptation, and sustainable stormwater management 
 
2.6.13.​
Cemetery/Memorial Park 
A 150% increase in cemetery and memorial park land is proposed—expanding from 7.37 ha to 18.41 ha. This proactive measure anticipates growing demand, 
ensuring the municipality maintains adequate space for dignified interments while contemplating long-term capacity needs. 
 
2.6.14.​
Buffer/Greenbelt 
Buffer and greenbelt zones are established along legal easements adjacent to water bodies—typically rivers, streams, lakes, or coastlines—to create a protected 
vegetated corridor. This corridor serves multiple purposes: 
-​
Water quality protection: Vegetated buffers filter out sediment, nutrients, pollutants, and pesticides from surface runoff before they reach water bodies, 
significantly enhancing aquatic health 
-​
Erosion control & flood mitigation: Deep-rooted trees, shrubs, and grasses stabilize banks, slow runoff, and mitigate erosion and flood impacts 
-​
Habitat & biodiversity support: These zones act as wildlife corridors and refuges, supporting diverse flora and fauna and enhancing ecological 
resilience . 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
211 


===== PAGE 215 =====
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
   
 
-​
Recreational and amenity value: Buffer strips provide scenic open spaces for trails, passive recreation, and community enjoyment 
-​
Legal & planning basis: In the Philippines, legal easement standards mandate protection strips—typically 3 m in urban, 20 m in agricultural, and 40 m 
in forest areas—along banks, to preserve banks and support drainage, recreation, navigation, and habitat 
2.6.15.​
Utilities, Transportation, and Services 
A 77% increase in land designated for urbanization and industrial purposes is proposed—expanding from 134.68 ha to 199.15 ha—to proactively respond to 
growing urban and industrial pressures within the municipality. 
 
-​
Anticipating urban growth: With expanding population and economic activity, municipalities must reserve adequate land for employment zones, 
housing, infrastructure, and essential services—ensuring orderly, efficient development 
-​
Fostering agglomeration benefits: Concentrating industrial and urban functions encourages productivity, reduces transportation costs, and supports 
innovation—key outcomes of well-managed urban spatial expansion 
-​
Integrated and sustainable zoning: This increase supports mixed-use and industrial zoning approaches that blend economic vitality with 
environmental safeguards, echoing global best practices in sustainable urban planning . 
-​
Mitigating urban sprawl: By proactively allocating land in strategic areas, the plan helps contain sprawl, lowers infrastructure costs, and supports 
compact growth—elements central to UN‑Habitat’s sustainable urbanization goals 
 
2.6.16.​
Planned Unit Development 
A Planned Unit Development (PUD) is a flexible, site-specific zoning tool that allows the incorporation of mixed land uses - residential, commercial, 
recreational, even light-industrial-within a unified, master-planned area. Rather than applying rigid, traditional zoning rules, the municipality works closely 
with developers to negotiate a comprehensive development plan that aligns with both investor goals and community needs. 
Anticipate investor needs: PUDs provide design and land-use flexibility, enabling investors to craft optimal layouts and amenity mixes tailored to their 
projects .​
 
Serve diverse market demands: By allowing mixed-use zones and varying housing types (e.g., single-family homes, townhouses, apartments), PUDs 
accommodate emerging housing and commercial preferences . 
 
Promote sustainability: Developers are incentivized to preserve open space, integrate green infrastructure, and prioritize walkability—helping Bamban 
realize its vision of ecological stability . 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

2.7.​ Land and Water Use Policies 
A land and water use policy refers to a statement indicating general principles or norms that will guide all development activities in the municipality to support 
and further encourage actions toward the achievement of the municipality’s vision, goals, and objectives. 
 
Moral and Godly. All land uses in the Municipality must be moral and Godly.  
General Welfare Test. All land uses must also meet the general welfare test: will this land use promote and protect the health, safety, peace, comfort, convenience, 
and general welfare of the local residents? 
 
Intergenerational Equity Test. All land uses must meet the intergenerational equity test: will it meet the needs of the present without compromising the needs 
of future generations? 
 
Response to CCA-DRR. The Municipality must protect the life of each resident by requiring adaptive and mitigating measures in houses and other structures 
in response to climate change and disaster risks. 
 
Food Self-Sufficiency. The Municipality must protect the minimum required agricultural land for food self-sufficiency for the next nine years. This primarily 
includes land cultivated with rice, but it also includes land with corn, vegetables, and other staple crops. 
 
Water Resources. All municipal waters in the municipality must be conserved and protected. All water easements must be kept clear from permanent 
structures.  
 
Cultural Conservation. The Municipality’s cultural heritage must be strictly protected, conserved, or preserved. This includes local traditions and festivities. 
 
Social Justice and Inclusivity. The Municipality must promote social justice or distributive justice by promoting the creation of cottage industries and 
MSMEs, by aggressively pursuing socialized housing goals, and by strictly enforcing the required socialized housing share in residential development projects. 
 
Aesthetics. The Municipality must promote an aesthetic and orderly arrangement of its urban areas by prohibiting or regulating the location of specific land 
uses. 
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

2.7.1.​
Environmental Sector Policies 
1.​ Protect natural waterways. Preserve and promote buffer zones, easements, and greenbelts. Secure for the residents of Bamban of present and future 
generations the perpetual existence of all native plants and animals. 
2.​ Afford full protection and advancement of the right of the people to a balanced and healthful ecology in accord with the rhythm and harmony of nature. 
3.​ Adopt a systematic, comprehensive, and ecological solid waste management program that shall ensure public health protection, as well as environmental 
protection. Ensure the proper management of solid and other wastes from communities, production areas, infrastructure areas, and life support areas. 
4.​ Protect, preserve, and revive the quality of municipal waters. 
5.​ Protect and preserve the quality of air in the municipality. 
6.​ Ensure the resilience of the natural environment to climate change and natural disasters.  
7.​ Reduce the vulnerability of the banks of waterways to climate change impacts and natural disasters. 
 
2.7.2.​
Social Sector Policies 
1.​ Ensure that everyone lives in a climate- and disaster-resilient house and community. 
2.​ Ensure that everyone lives in comfortable, convenient, gender-inclusive, culturally diverse, age-friendly, healthy, and accessible communities. 
3.​ Promote lifelong learning for all ages and provide high-quality, accessible, inclusive, and affordable education for all. 
4.​ Promote social justice and full human development through social welfare programs that will reduce poverty, improve living conditions, and enhance the 
dignity and welfare of every individual. 
5.​ Promote the total development of children including their physical, moral, spiritual, intellectual, and social well-being. Protect children from abuse, 
violence, exploitation, neglect, and other conditions prejudicial to their development. 
6.​ Provide a comprehensive program to ensure the rehabilitation, self-development, and self-reliance of disabled persons. 
7.​ Promote social justice and human dignity through poverty alleviation programs that will reduce poverty incidence among Filipinos. 
8.​ Ensure the sufficiency of parks and recreational spaces. Preserve and promote green open spaces in communities. Preserve and promote green open spaces 
in industrial estates and commercial districts. Preserve and promote green open spaces along streets and water bodies. 
9.​ Protect and promote culture through sustainable tourism and heritage conservation.  
10.​Ensure peace and order and public safety in production areas.  
11.​Uphold people’s constitutional rights to life and property by addressing disaster risks through effective preparedness, prevention, and mitigation 
measures. Reduce the vulnerability of communities to climate change impacts and natural disasters.

2.7.3.​
Economic Sector Policies 
1.​ Promote sustainability in agriculture, industry, commerce, and tourism. Ensure the resilience of the agricultural, industrial, commercial, and tourism 
sectors. Reduce the vulnerability of agricultural areas to climate change impacts and natural disasters. 
2.​ Support and promote the municipality’s food, recreational, financial, and other commercial establishments. 
3.​ Promote the development and use of information and communications technology to improve competitiveness. 
4.​ Foster development and promote inclusive growth through the development of micro, small, and medium enterprises. Promote, support, strengthen and 
encourage the growth and development of MSMEs. 
 
2.7.4.​
Infrastructure Sector Policies 
1.​ Promote the development of modern infrastructure to attract and maintain investments. 
2.​ Ensure efficient transportation to and from the municipality. 
3.​ Promote sustainable infrastructure development. Promote efficient public transportation and active transportation.  
4.​ Ensure the resilience of infrastructure to climate change and natural disasters. Reduce the vulnerability of roads, bridges, electrical facilities, water 
facilities and other infrastructure to climate change impacts and natural disasters. 
 
2.7.5.​
Institutional Sector Policies 
1.​ Promote a high standard of ethics in public service. Promote integrity, accountability, proper management of public affairs and public property, and to 
establish effective practices aimed at the prevention of graft and corruption in government. 
2.​ Promote efficient delivery of government services by reducing bureaucratic red tape, preventing graft and corruption, and improving ease of doing 
business in the city. 
3.​ Value the dignity of every human person and guarantee full respect for human rights regardless of race, religion or belief, ethnicity, gender identity, sexual 
orientation or preference, age, disability, or other status.

■​ 2.8.1 On-going Projects 
"Rehabilitation and Development of the Japanese World War II Tunnels: A Collaborative Project with the Provincial Government of 
Tarlac" 
This project focuses on the comprehensive rehabilitation and development of the Japanese World War II tunnels, a significant historical landmark located 
in the province of Tarlac. In partnership with the Provincial Government of Tarlac, the initiative aims to preserve and restore the tunnels, which serve as a 
testament to the historical events that unfolded during the Japanese occupation of the Philippines. 
The primary goal of this endeavor is not only to safeguard these tunnels as a cultural and historical heritage site but also to enhance its accessibility and 
educational value. The project will involve structural repairs, preservation of original features, and the integration of modern facilities to ensure the site’s 
long-term sustainability as a tourist destination and educational resource. 
Through this rehabilitation, the project seeks to honor the sacrifices made during World War II and provide future generations with an opportunity to 
learn about this pivotal period in Philippine history. Additionally, it will contribute to the economic development of the region by boosting local tourism, 
providing employment opportunities, and fostering a deeper understanding of the province's rich historical heritage. 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

"Rehabilitation and Improvement of the Bamban Municipal Hall: A Step Towards Modernizing Local Governance and Community Services" 
The Rehabilitation and Improvement of the Bamban Municipal Hall project is a critical initiative aimed at enhancing the functionality, accessibility, and 
aesthetic appeal of the local government’s administrative hub. This project will focus on restoring and modernizing the existing structure to better serve the needs 
of the local community while preserving the historical significance of the municipal hall. 
The rehabilitation will involve comprehensive upgrades to the building's infrastructure, including structural repairs, enhanced facilities, and the integration of 
modern technologies. This will ensure that the Municipal Hall can continue to function efficiently as a space for government services, community engagement, 
and public events. Additionally, improvements will include accessibility enhancements to ensure that the hall meets the needs of all residents, including senior 
citizens and persons with disabilities. 
By revitalizing this central government facility, the project will not only improve the delivery of public services but also promote a more conducive environment 
for local governance. The improved Municipal Hall will serve as a symbol of progress and innovation, reinforcing the commitment of the local government to 
meet the growing demands of the community. 
This project will also have a positive impact on the local economy by providing job opportunities during the construction phase and creating a more attractive 
and efficient government service center that can better support Bamban’s residents and visitors.
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​

"Creation of the 4PH Housing Project in the Municipality of Bamban: Addressing the Growing Housing Backlog and Providing Sustainable 
Resettlement Solutions" 
The creation of the 4PH Housing Project in 
the Municipality of Bamban is an essential 
initiative aimed at addressing the increasing 
housing backlog, particularly in light of the 
long-term challenges stemming from the 
eruption of Mount Pinatubo. In the aftermath 
of the 1991 eruption, countless families were 
displaced from their homes, with many initially 
relocated to the DapDap Resettlement area. 
However, some families chose to resettle in 
government-owned properties, such as along the 
Philippine National Railway (PNR) and Old 
Anupul Road, while others sought shelter on 
abandoned or deserted lands. 
This housing project, to be located in Barangay 
Anupul is designed to provide a permanent and 
sustainable solution for these displaced families, 
offering them secure, well-planned housing 
options and an improved quality of life. The 
4PH project will focus on developing land with 
proper infrastructure and services, ensuring that 
the families who have struggled with inadequate 
shelter and lack of basic utilities for years will 
now have access to clean water, electricity, roads, 
and safe living spaces. 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
234 


===== PAGE 238 =====
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
   
 
The project will also aim to address the broader housing backlog in the municipality, which has continued to grow over time as Bamban’s population has 
increased. By providing affordable and accessible housing options, the initiative will not only give displaced families the chance to rebuild their lives but will also 
contribute to the overall development of the municipality, fostering community stability and prosperity. 
Furthermore, the 4PH Housing Project will incorporate features of sustainable urban planning, ensuring that it meets environmental and social standards while 
promoting long-term resilience. The project will involve collaboration between local government agencies, national entities, and private stakeholders to create a 
comprehensive approach to addressing the housing needs of Bamban's residents. 
Through this project, the Municipality of Bamban is committed to providing its residents with secure and sustainable homes, contributing to the municipality’s 
vision of a more inclusive, thriving, and resilient community.
