2.8.2 Pipeline Projects 
 
"Rehabilitation and Improvement of Lourdes Grotto and 
Park: 
Preserving 
Cultural 
Heritage 
and 
Enhancing 
Community Recreation" 
The Rehabilitation and Improvement of Lourdes Grotto and 
Park is a transformative project aimed at revitalizing one of the 
Municipality’s most cherished landmarks. Lourdes Grotto and Park, 
which has long served as a spiritual and recreational site for residents 
and visitors alike, will undergo significant improvements to enhance 
its beauty, accessibility, and functionality, ensuring that it remains a 
center for reflection, recreation, and community engagement. 
This project will focus on restoring and preserving the historical and 
religious significance of the Lourdes Grotto, while also enhancing 
the surrounding park area to provide a more welcoming and 
enjoyable space for public use. The rehabilitation efforts will include 
structural repairs, landscaping upgrades, and the installation of 
modern amenities such as lighting, seating areas, and pathways, all 
designed to ensure a safe and pleasant experience for visitors. 
The aim of the rehabilitation is twofold: first, to honor and protect 
the spiritual value of the Lourdes Grotto as a place of pilgrimage and 
quiet contemplation, and second, to transform the surrounding park 
into a vibrant, multifunctional public space that can accommodate 
recreational activities, local events, and family gatherings. The park’s 
improvements will include better facilities for outdoor events, 
improved green spaces, and enhanced areas for rest and leisure, 
making it an ideal destination for people of all ages.

Additionally, the project will prioritize environmental sustainability,

Through this rehabilitation and improvement initiative, the Lourdes 
Grotto and Park will not only continue to serve as a spiritual and

this iconic landmark and enhancing its facilities, the project will 
contribute to the overall development of the municipality, 
encouraging tourism, fostering a deeper connection to local heritage, 
and providing a serene environment for reflection and recreation.

"Creation of the 'Road to Pinatubo' and the 'Pinatubo Tourist Center with View Deck': Enhancing Access to Natural Wonders and Boosting Local 
Tourism" 
The 'Road to Pinatubo' and the 'Pinatubo Tourist Center with View Deck' project is a visionary initiative aimed at enhancing access to one of the 
Philippines' most iconic natural landmarks—the Mount Pinatubo Crater. This project will provide improved infrastructure and facilities that will make it easier 
for both local and international tourists to experience the beauty and significance of Mount Pinatubo, while also promoting the region as a premier ecotourism 
destination. 
The development of the 'Road to Pinatubo' will involve the construction of a modern, safe, and well-maintained road that leads directly to the base of Mount 
Pinatubo, improving access for visitors and enhancing the travel experience. This new road will reduce travel time, ensure safer journeys for tourists, and provide a 
smoother, more enjoyable ride through the scenic landscapes of Tarlac, Pampanga, and Zambales. The road will also incorporate necessary infrastructure, such as 
proper signage, rest stops, and safety features, to ensure a comfortable and secure trip for visitors. 
The Pinatubo Tourist Center with View Deck will be designed as a modern, multifunctional hub that will serve as the main point of arrival for visitors. The 
center will offer a variety of services, including information about the region’s history, the eruption of Mount Pinatubo, local culture, and available tour packages. 
The inclusion of a view deck will allow visitors to take in stunning panoramic views of the crater lake and the surrounding volcanic terrain, creating an 
unforgettable experience that will highlight the natural beauty of the area. The center will also feature amenities such as restrooms, food stalls, souvenir shops, 
and seating areas to provide comfort and convenience for tourists. 
This project will not only improve tourism infrastructure but also promote the ecological and cultural significance of Mount Pinatubo. The development will 
contribute to the sustainable growth of the local economy by creating jobs in construction, tourism, and hospitality, as well as generating income for nearby 
communities through increased visitation. Furthermore, the project will create opportunities for the promotion of environmental awareness, ensuring that 
visitors understand the importance of preserving the natural landscape and biodiversity of the region. 
Through the Road to Pinatubo and the Pinatubo Tourist Center with View Deck, this initiative will enhance the region's tourism appeal, providing a 
gateway for travelers to explore the natural wonders of Mount Pinatubo, while fostering a deeper appreciation for the cultural and historical significance of the 
area. By improving accessibility and amenities, the project aims to make the region a more attractive and sustainable destination for both local and international 
visitors.

2.9.​ Development Programs and Projects Highlights 
2.9.1.​
Environment Sector 
●​ The municipal MRF must have sufficient space and must be accessible to built-up areas. 
●​ Ensure the sufficiency of parks and recreational areas in all barangays. 
●​ The municipality must promote an aesthetic and orderly arrangement of its urban areas by prohibiting or regulating the location of specific land uses. 
●​ All municipal waters in the municipality must be conserved and protected. All water easements must be kept clear from permanent structures. 
●​ Prevent the construction of permanent structures in the forests and mountain areas of Bamban. 
●​ Prevent the construction of buildings for businesses or industries with significant GHG emissions and negative environmental impact. 
●​ Prohibit the hunting of vulnerable species, promote the rewilding of forests, and encourage the planting or re-planting of diversified native trees. 
 
2.9.2.​
Social Sector 
●​ Health facilities must be accessible to residential areas. 
●​ Educational facilities must be accessible to residential areas. 
●​ Social welfare facilities must be accessible to residential areas. 
●​ Educational and recreational facilities must be accessible to residential areas. 
●​ All land uses in the municipality must be moral and Godly.  
●​ All land uses must also meet the general welfare test: “Will this land use promote and protect the health, safety, peace, comfort, convenience, and 
general welfare of Bambanenses?” 
●​ Protective services must be accessible to all residential areas. 
●​ Residences must not be located in areas that are highly susceptible to flood, landslide, and other hazards. 
●​ The municipality must protect the life of each Bambanense by requiring adaptive and mitigating measures in houses and other structures in response 
to climate change and disaster risks.

Other Implementation Tools and Instruments 
There are other tools that the municipality can use to implement the CLUP and enforce the zoning ordinance such as the idle land tax. 
 
2.10.2.1.​
Idle Land tax 
Land price speculation and idle properties may be discouraged through maximizing the idle land tax (maximum of 5% as provided for in R.A. 7160, and 
reinforced by DILG-DOF JMC No. 2012-02). This tax provides other benefits: 
•​
Helps ensure that lands are utilized based on the principle of highest and best use (and more importantly, the most sustainable use) 
•​
Encourages landowners to continuously develop and maintain real properties 
•​
Reduces the possibility of urban blight and decay 
•​
Provides additional revenues to the municipality 
•​
Sustains the delivery of basic services to local residents

2.11.​ Communication Plan 
2.11.1.​
Rationale 
The Comprehensive Land Use Plan (CLUP) is an indispensable plan where thematic plans of the LGUS are hinged on. It has also been referred to as the “mother 
plan” as it is the progenitor of other plans. The essence of the CLUP is the judicious allocation of finite land resources within the territorial boundaries of a city 
or municipality to the different needs of various sectors, overarched by the local vision for development. 
Corollary to this, it can be said that the vision of the CLUP recognizes the conflicting demands on land such as: 
●​ Food security – the LGU must protect its remaining agricultural land to support food self-sufficiency and security. 
●​ Housing – the LGU must ensure that there is enough land for residential development considering the projected growth in population. 
●​ Environmental protection – the LGU must ensure ecological balance by protecting water bodies and forests. 
To avoid and mitigate such conflicts, and to unite and direct all stakeholders towards the shared vision and common goals, the implementation of this CLUP 
should include a communication plan. This is a critical component of the CLUP which aims to sufficiently and even fully inform various stakeholders not only 
of the CLUP’s importance, purpose, and regulations (through the Zoning Ordinance), but also of each stakeholder’s roles and responsibilities towards the 
realization of the land use plan.

2.12.​ Monitoring, Review, and Evaluation 
2.12.1.​
MRE for CLUP and ZO 
2.12.1.1.​
Monitoring 
The purpose of monitoring in the context of this plan is for the collection of data required for reviewing the implementation or execution of the CLUP and ZO. 
Data that are essential for the review and evaluation of the Comprehensive Land Use Plan and the Zoning Ordinance shall be monitored continuously by the 
municipality’s respective departments or offices. For example, violations of prescribed building height limits should be monitored by the Municipal Engineer’s 
Office. 
 
These inputs shall then be collected, verified, compiled, summarized, and reported by the Municipal Planning and Development Office (MPDO) on a monthly 
basis. The MPDO shall maintain a Management Information System (MIS) which includes standard input forms and databases that may be used by all of the 
municipality’s departments and offices to facilitate continuous data collection.  
 
A shared Geographic Information System (GIS) shall also form part of the MIS. This shall contain updated cadastral maps and data from the Municipal 
Assessor’s Office in addition to the existing geographical data collected, analyzed, and generated by the MPDO. 
 
2.12.1.2.​
Review 
A monthly report shall be submitted to the MRE Committee for a cursory review. The report shall be submitted in a standard format designed by the MPDO 
and pre-approved by the MRE Committee such as a “CLUP/ZO Report Card” that shows status or progress compared with baseline indicators. 
 
 
Bamban Comprehensive Land Use Plan (2022-2031) Volume 1: The Land Use Plan​​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
249 


===== PAGE 253 =====
​
​
​
​
​
​
​
   
 
Significant issues and concerns highlighted by the monthly report may prompt the MRE Committee to call for an interim review of the implementation of the 
CLUP and ZO. However, in the absence of any significant issue or concern, the MRE Committee shall review the implementation of the CLUP and ZO 
annually, preferably within the last quarter of the year. 
 
The purpose of review is not to alter the CLUP and ZO, but to either improve the implementation procedures, identify any addition or modification of 
CLUP-related programs and projects, or recommend legislations to strengthen implementation. 
 
2.12.1.3.​
Evaluation 
A three-year report aggregating the monthly reports shall be prepared by the MPDO for an evaluation by the MRE Committee. The purpose of evaluation is to 
determine whether parts of the CLUP or ZO require any major change, or whether the CLUP or the ZO is due for an overall update. 
 
2.12.2.​
MRE for Projects 
2.12.2.1.​
Monitoring 
In the context of project management, monitoring shall mean the continuous tracking of a CLUP-related project’s progress (percent constructed, percent 
disbursed/spent, etc.) versus planned milestones or standards. Monitoring includes collecting, measuring, and assessing measurements and trends to effect process 
improvements. Continuous monitoring gives the LGU insights into the health of each project and identifies any areas that may require special attention. 
CLUP-related projects shall be monitored by the MPDO’s Monitoring and Evaluation Division. 
 
2.12.2.2.​
Review 
A periodic review or an “on-going evaluation” shall be conducted by the MPDO’s Monitoring and Evaluation Division to assess any required intervention or 
modification to an on-going project.

2.12.2.3.​
Evaluation 
An evaluation, in the context of this plan, is an assessment that is as systematic and impartial as possible. It aims to determine the relevance, impact, effectiveness, 
efficiency, and sustainability of each completed project. The MPDO’s Monitoring and Evaluation Section shall conduct three types of evaluation: 
1.​ Terminal evaluation – conducted upon project completion to determine whether the project meets the agreed-upon standards and specifications. 
2.​ Ex-post evaluation – may be conducted at least three years from project completion to determine the project’s performance and assess its effectivity 
and sustainability. 
3.​ Impact evaluation – may be conducted at a longer time frame (say, six or nine years) and focuses on the impact of the project on the local 
community and whether it has contributed to the achievement of the municipality’s development goals. 
The last two evaluations may be outsourced by the MPDO to third-party auditors for a more objective assessment. 
 
2.12.3.​
Reporting 
The MPDO’s Monitoring and Evaluation Section shall submit a monthly status report of all CLUP-related projects to the MRE Committee and may 
recommend appropriate actions, interventions, or modifications to significant issues and concerns.

2.12.4.​
Creation of an MRE Committee 
Under the LGC, the Local Development Council (LDC) is responsible for LGU plan preparation as well as monitoring, review, and evaluation (MRE).  
 
Within the LDC, an MRE body shall be created whose membership shall be identified and functions defined. An MRE Committee shall be organized to 
undertake the monitoring, review, and evaluation of the CLUP and ZO implementation. 
 
The lead technical and implementing arm shall have the same composition as the Local Zoning Review Committee as follows: 
1.​ Sangguniang Bayan Chairperson on Land Use  
2.​ Municipal Planning and Development Officer 
3.​ Municipal Zoning Administrator  
4.​ Municipal Assessor 
5.​ Municipal Legal Officer 
6.​ Municipal Engineer 
7.​ Municipal Environment and Natural Resources Officer 
8.​ Municipal Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Officer 
9.​ Municipal Agricultural Officer 
10.​ President, Liga ng Barangay (LnB) or Association of Barangay Captains (ABC) 
11.​ Three (3) Private Sector Representatives such as from the academe 
12.​ Two (2) non-government and civil society organization representatives 
The MPDO’s Monitoring and Evaluation Section shall serve as the secretariat to the MRE Committee. It shall also specify the functions of each committee 
member and recommend the addition of other thematic areas and members. 
 
At least 40% of the committee members should be female.
